# DSWD DROMIC Report #52 on Typhoon “Nika”, Super Typhoon “Ofel”, and Super Typhoon “Pepito”

as of 05 February 2025, 6PM

## I. Situation Overview

On 09 November 2024 at around 8 AM, the Low Pressure Area east of Southern Luzon developed into Tropical Depression (TD) “Nika”. At around 11 PM, “Nika” intensified into a Tropical Storm (TS).

On 10 November 2024 at around 5 AM, “Nika” further strengthened into a Severe Tropical Storm (STS) and underwent rapid intensification.

On 11 November 2024 at around 5 AM, “Nika” intensified into a Typhoon (TY), over the sea east of Aurora and made landfall in the vicinity of Dilasag, Aurora at around 8:10 AM. At around 8 PM, “Nika” weakened into an STS as it moved over the coasta waters of Ilocos Sur.

While “Nika” further moved northwestward over the West Philippine Sea on 12 November 2024 at 3 AM, TS “Ofel” entered the Philippine Area of Responsibility (PAR). “Nika” exited PAR at around 2 PM on the same day, while “Ofel” strengthened into STS category.

On 13 November 2024 at around 5 AM, “Ofel” further intensified into a TY and became a Super Typhoon (STY) on 14 November 2024 at around 8 AM. By 1:30 PM, the center of its eye made landfall in the vicinity of Baggao, Cagayan and weakened into a TY. On the same day, STS “Manyi” (international name) entered PAR at around 8 PM and was given the domestic name “Pepito”.

On 15 November 2024 at around 5 AM, “Pepito” intensified into a TY. At around 5 PM, TY “Ofel” weakened into STS category and exited PAR. At around 11 PM, STS “Ofel” re-entered PAR west northwest of Batanes while “Pepito” further intensified as it continued to move west northwestward over the Philippine Sea east of Eastern Visayas.

On 16 November 2024 at around 5 AM, “Ofel” continuously weakened as it moved slowly over the sea of Taiwan while “Pepito” moved towards Southern Luzon and Eastern Visayas as it nears STY category. By 11 AM, “Pepito” intensified into an STY and made landfall over Panganiban, Catanduanes at around 9:40 PM. At 11 PM, the Philippine Atmospheric, Geophysical and Astronomical Services Administration (PAGASA) reported that “Ofel” no longer affects the land areas of the country.

On 17 November 2024 at around 5 AM, PAGASA reported life-threatening conditions over the eastern section of Southern Luzon as “Pepito” moved over the sea of East of Bicol Region. At around 3:20 PM, “Pepito” made its second landfall in the vicinity of Dipaculao, Aurora. It weakened into a TY while moving over Nueva Vizcaya at 8 PM. On 18 November 2024 at 2 AM, “Pepito” further weakened and accelerated northwestward over the West Philippine Sea. “Pepito” exited PAR at around 12 PM.

Source: DOST-PAGASA

## II. Status of Affected Areas and Population

A total of 1,388,221 families or 5,084,774 persons are affected in 8,806 barangays in Regions I, II, III, CALABARZON, MIMAROPA, V, VIII, and CAR (see Annex A).

## III. Status of Displaced Population

## a. Inside Evacuation Center

There are 80 families or 277 persons currently taking temporary shelter in 13 evacuation centers in Regions III, and V (see Annex B).

## b. Outside Evacuation Center

There are 1,848 families or 6,144 persons temporarily staying with their relatives or friends (see Annex E).

## c. Total Displaced Population

There are 1,928 families or 6,421 persons still displaced in Regions III, and V (see Annex F).

## IV. Damaged Houses

A total of 122,824 houses were damaged; of which, 20,254 are totally damaged and 102,570 are partially damaged in Regions I, II, III, CALABARZON, V, VIII, and CAR (see Annex G).

## V. Cost of Humanitarian Assistance Provided

A total of ₱856,016,787.97 worth of assistance was provided to the affected families composed of ₱828,498,881.86 from DSWD, ₱23,961,780.53 from the Local Government Units (LGUs), 3,494,545.58 from Non-Government Organizations (NGOs), and ₱61,580.00 from other Partners (see Annex H).

## 1. Standby Funds

• ₱3.30 million Quick Response Fund (QRF) available at the DSWD Central Office.  
• ₱24 million available at DSWD FOs I, II, III, CALABARZON, MIMAROPA, V, VIII, and CAR.  
₱20 million available in other DSWD FOs which may support the relief needs of the families affected by Tropical Cyclones “Nika”, “Ofel”, and “Pepito”.

## 2. Prepositioned FFPs and Other Relief Items

393,738 FFPs available in Disaster Response Centers; of which, 172,156 FFPs are at the National Resource Operations Center (NROC), Pasay City and 221,582FFPs are at the Visayas Disaster Resource Center (VDRC), Mandaue City.  
1,071,755 FFPs available at DSWD FOs I, II, III, CALABARZON, MIMAROPA, V, VIII, and CAR.  
850,703 FFPs in other DSWD FOs which may support the relief needs of the families affected by Tropical Cyclones “Nika”, “Ofel” and “Pepito”.  
867.64 million worth of other FNIs at NROC, VDRC, and DSWD Fos warehouses.
